# Issue #79 — PR #103 blocking-fix ledger

This additive writer ledger covers the eight P1 findings in the
[Codex review comment](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6172592368)
against immutable source `1407f88a58a796a7bec8b1736ca1e179dc8678d3`.
The local correction starts at `80ee23053b53f94dacc35fd09d8a51546b2ca5e3`.
Authorship follows the current explicit override: Codex `gpt-6.1-sol`, medium.
This is writer evidence, not independent or exact-head GitHub review approval.
Prior finding dispositions remain in the packet's historical evidence at the
immutable starting head; the [local continuation](issue79-local-continuation-2026-10-15.md)
records the earlier runtime correction. This ledger adds the current review
delta and does not replace those records or assert new approval of them.

Every finding below is blocking P1, reproduced using inert source passed to
`inspect_python_heredoc`, then corrected locally. No specimen, packet recipe,
malicious string or returned callable was executed. Only inspected trusted
scanner/harness definitions executed, under CPython 3.14.3 with `-I -B` and an
allowlisted environment containing only `PATH` and `LC_ALL`. No credentials,
live runner operations, GitHub writes, push, PR mutation or merge were used.

| Finding in the linked immutable review | Red evidence | Local resolution and focused evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Propagate arguments into lambda and method parameters | Inline and assigned lambda keys, instance method keys and static method keys were accepted. | Bind arguments to visible lambdas and resolved methods, preserving keyword/default/variadic flow and static versus bound parameters. `test_pr103_lambda_method_error_arguments` rejects all four forms and accepts a literal-key lambda. |
| Follow mutations through mapping aliases | Five mutation forms through cyclic aliases were accepted before iterating the original mapping. | Compute a scoped, bidirectional alias closure for recorded keys, with set union terminating cycles. `test_pr103_mapping_alias_mutations` covers subscript store, `update`, `setdefault`, `__setitem__` and `|=`; literal keys remain accepted. Existing unrelated-function and environment-key positives remain green. |
| Track conversion callables stored in dictionaries | Dictionary-held `int`, `float` and `complex` conversions of a sensitive operand were accepted. | Resolve literal dictionary keys precisely; conservatively inspect all values for unresolved keys/unpacks. `test_pr103_dictionary_conversion_callables` rejects all three conversions and accepts literal input and a known nonconversion dictionary entry. |
| Follow factory-returned keys into repr checks | Direct and chained factory-returned custom keys were accepted. | Follow local function, lambda and method returns before inspecting class representation methods, with visited-node guards. `test_pr103_factory_returned_repr_keys` rejects both factory depths and accepts a safe representation. Existing direct/inherited/parameter key controls remain green. |
| Traverse comprehensions in launcher defaults | List, set, generator and dictionary comprehensions containing launchers were accepted. | Inspect yielded expressions, iterable sources and filters; mark launcher-bearing unresolved defaults so direct and subscript calls fail closed. `test_pr103_comprehension_launcher_defaults` covers six forms, including subprocess workflow argv, and accepts a `len` default. Existing direct/nested default controls remain green. |
| Reject sensitive names passed to attribute lookup | Direct `getattr` and `delattr` sensitive names were accepted. | Apply operand sensitivity to attribute-name arguments, including assigned callable aliases. `test_pr103_sensitive_attribute_names` rejects both direct and alias forms and accepts a literal name. |
| Classify launchers imported into class namespaces | Class-qualified from-import launcher aliases were accepted. | Retain qualified launcher provenance for class `ImportFrom` and `Import` bindings. `test_pr103_class_namespace_launchers` rejects OS and subprocess forms, retains the already-rejected class module form, and accepts an allowed `re` module member. |
| Trace custom display methods at output sinks | Custom display objects at `print`, `sys.exit` and factory-result sinks were accepted. | Follow local display-method returns, inherited methods, factories, container entries and explicit string/format wrappers at sinks. `test_pr103_display_methods_at_sinks` covers `__str__`, `__repr__`, `__format__`, direct/factory arguments and string/f-string wrappers; safe display remains accepted. All three protocols are conservatively considered at these sinks, even when a particular sink would choose only one. |

## Actual validation of this correction

Commands below use this common prefix, with a PATH/locale-only environment:

`env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py`

- Initial RED: `-k test_pr103` ran 8 methods in 0.391s and reported 34
  failures: 33 negative subcases plus one invalid positive control importing an
  already-disallowed module. That control was corrected to an allowed `re`
  class member; import policy was not relaxed. The class module-import negative
  was already rejected. Direct attribute-name assertions failed before the
  alias assertions could run.
- First correction: the same 8 methods passed in 0.343s. Valid set/generator
  launcher invocations then replaced placeholder indexing, and extra explicit
  string/f-string display probes produced 6 RED subcases in 2 methods (0.252s).
  Those wrappers were then included in display traversal.
- The first broader run exposed 7 regressions in existing nested launcher
  defaults after introducing the container marker. Direct calls to that marker
  were corrected; it already rejected subscript calls.
- Two broader invocations were stopped by targeting only their owned test
  processes after the scanner self-audit became slow; no final timing or pass
  is claimed for those interrupted invocations (both exit 143). The second
  process was observed at 2m27s elapsed before termination. Factory/display
  traversal was changed to a worklist so shared return/parameter graphs expand
  each node once, then given an early return when no local class exists.
- Focused run before that worklist refactor: 19 methods passed in 0.454s.
  Exact selectors are the eight
  `test_pr103_*` methods above plus:
  `test_launchers_bound_through_callable_defaults_stay_classified`,
  `test_launchers_nested_in_default_expressions_stay_classified`,
  `test_sensitive_operands_are_rejected_before_implicit_exception_messages`,
  `test_complex_conversions_are_rejected_before_implicit_exception_messages`,
  `test_conversion_callables_in_defaults_and_containers_are_tracked`,
  `test_sensitive_call_arguments_propagate_into_parameters`,
  `test_late_inserted_sensitive_keys_lose_keys_only_exemption`,
  `test_keys_with_sensitive_representations_are_rejected`,
  `test_sensitive_values_are_tainted_into_method_and_lambda_parameters`,
  `test_sensitive_taint_crosses_inline_lambda_and_static_method_parameters`,
  `test_sensitive_mapping_return_survives_unrelated_nested_name_collision`.
  All selectors are methods of `Issue79RegressionTests`.
- After the worklist/no-class and no-local-method fast returns, those 19
  focused methods passed in 0.462s. The method fast return avoids searching
  factory/alias graphs when the indexed tree contains no local method at all;
  it does not remove any possible local method candidate.
- Coordinator-requested adjacent probes both reproduced RED: constructor alias
  `Alias = K; print(Alias())` and an unpacked conversion dictionary,
  2 methods / 2 failures in 0.233s. The constructor resolver now follows scoped
  class aliases, and the conversion resolver follows dictionary unpack values.
  `test_pr103_display_constructor_aliases` and
  `test_pr103_unpacked_dictionary_converters` include safe counterparts.
  All 10 `test_pr103_*` methods then passed in 0.560s.
- Final focused run after the adjacent corrections: 21 methods passed in
  0.448s, using the 10 `test_pr103_*` methods plus the 11 prior selectors listed
  above. The longer preceding-source invocation also completed: 20 methods
  passed in 528.091s, including
  `test_current_scanner_compiler_body_remains_self_audit_compatible`. That run
  loaded source before the no-local-method fast return and the two adjacent
  corrections; it is not final-source evidence. It was allowed to finish.

The final-source packet smoke uses selector
`Issue79RegressionTests.test_current_packet_has_no_static_scanner_violations`
with the same isolated/environment prefix. Its inputs were frozen at packet Git
blob `95e8f0cac90165e45827e645800acf67b8abe566` and harness Git blob
`18aff0dfeb6b89998ed3ae37850d6b22737b7661`; later ledger edits do not alter either
input. This selector inspects packet recipes as data and never runs them.
It ran once on those inputs and passed: 1 test in 537.667s, exit 0;
261 shell commands and 86 Python heredoc bodies inspected, zero violations.
The final hashes were checked again after completion. `git diff --check` and
scans of the introduced delta/ledger for personal machine paths and credential
patterns passed. No full regression suite was run or repeated by this writer.

requests, final finding triage and exact-head merge authorization. No full

These checks do not complete G01/G02 or authorize production or live work.

Rollback is a reviewed revert of the local correction commit. Historical packet
evidence, toolchain pins, Go code and shared CI are unchanged.

## Additive correction from `ca6af5676548ffe716ca3a2d35fea07f7f71c64d`

The seven blocking P1 classes in immutable
[review comment 6338125927](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6338125927)
were reproduced and corrected by the sole writer using the current explicit
Codex `gpt-6.1-sol`, medium override. The preceding eight-plus-two corrections,
21-method focused pass and 537.667s packet scan above remain historical evidence
for their recorded inputs; they did not establish closure of these seven gaps.
The starting head had an independent HOLD despite a passed hosted quick check.
This additive section records writer evidence, not review or merge approval.

| Class | Reproduction and correction | Controls retained |
|---|---|---|
| A: unbound method arguments | `test_pr103_delta_unbound_method_arguments` reproduces both an explicit `None` receiver followed by a sensitive key and a sensitive explicit `self`. Class receivers retain the first argument; instance receivers and classmethod descriptors consume their implicit receiver, while static methods retain all explicit arguments. Assigned method aliases use the same receiver check. | Literal-key bound/unbound calls and static methods; existing sensitive method/lambda controls. |
| B: mapping identity across helper/closure scopes | `test_pr103_delta_mapping_identity` reproduces five helper-parameter mutation forms and a direct outer-mapping closure mutation. Scoped identity edges now include call-bound parameters and resolve direct mutation receivers to their lexical binding. A second RED exposed a helper-local alias of its parameter; that alias now joins the same identity graph. | A mutation of another mapping, unrelated same-name function scopes, and the existing closure-local alias. |
| C: named comprehension defaults | `test_pr103_delta_named_comprehension_defaults` follows a comprehension through `box` and another alias into a default, covering list/set/generator/dict forms. The existing launcher-default traversal now follows assigned Name sources with its visited guard. | Named `len` comprehension/default; set/generator unsafe forms already refused at baseline. |
| D: subscript constructor aliases | `test_pr103_delta_display_subscripts` reproduces tuple-extracted class aliases, including a named tuple. Constructor traversal now follows selected container entries. | Identical constructors returning a literal display string. |
| E: container-held display objects | The same method reproduces list and dictionary/subscript object sinks, including a container alias. Display traversal follows the selected entries before inspecting display methods. | Identical object/container shapes with literal display strings. |
| F: conversion dictionary alias chains | `test_pr103_delta_conversion_dictionary_aliases` reproduces `int`, `float`, and `complex` behind two aliases, both directly and through dictionary unpacking. A scoped container selector follows aliases before selecting keys; unresolved keys retain all alternatives. | Numeric literal input and known nonconversion keys; existing unpack/default/container controls. |
| G: class-imported launcher receivers | `test_pr103_delta_class_import_receivers` reproduces imported OS/subprocess launchers through class aliases, direct instances and named instances. Class-attribute classification resolves these receiver aliases before matching launcher provenance. | Equivalent `re.escape` class/instance calls and existing direct class-import controls. |

The container selector is shared by the existing conversion and display checks;
it adds no callable, module, path, variable-name or recipe allowlist exception.
The correction preserves conservative alternative bindings. Its subscript
recursion shares a visited-expression guard: self/mutual container-subscript
cycles terminate without discarding a reachable conversion alternative.
`test_pr103_delta_subscript_cycles_retain_alternatives` covers both sensitive
alternatives and a safe `len` cycle.

All commands in this section use the same PATH/locale-only prefix:

`env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py`

- Baseline RED, prefix plus `-k test_pr103_delta`: six methods, 26 failed unsafe
  assertions in 0.460s, exit 1. Safe counterparts remained accepted; the set and
  generator launcher cases were already refused. D/E share one test method.
- First GREEN, same command: six methods passed in 0.419s. Prefix plus
  `-k test_pr103` then passed all 16 methods in 0.594s.
- After the shared cycle guard was added during source inspection, a first
  adjacent-control run passed 27 methods in 0.905s. A subsequently added
  helper-local parameter alias reproduced RED with prefix plus
  `-k test_pr103_delta_mapping_identity`: one failure in 0.252s. Its scoped
  alias correction passed the same method in 0.431s.
- Coordinator independently identified the subscript-cycle risk before freeze.
  To record meaningful RED for the new cycle regression, only the recursive
  call's shared guard argument was temporarily removed, restoring fresh-set
  recursion. Prefix plus
  `-k test_pr103_delta_subscript_cycles_retain_alternatives` produced three
  `RecursionError` results in one method, 0.403s, exit 1. The shared guard was
  restored before final verification; this is incremental-source RED, not a
  claim that the original baseline contained this newly introduced resolver.
- Pre-freeze focused GREEN: 28 methods passed in 1.079s, exit 0. Exact suffix:

## Completed GitHub review additions on the same starting head

Before the correction was committed, GitHub Codex completed its review of
`ca6af5676548ffe716ca3a2d35fea07f7f71c64d` with eleven P1 findings in
[comment 6338293554](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6338293554).
The coordinator classified one duplicate of class A and ten distinct additions;
all eleven remain blocking until the corrected candidate receives its required
review. The writer read the complete comment, including via
`gh api repos/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/issues/comments/6338293554 --jq .body`.

The previous packet rerun on blob `a3bc6e2f59dd0c43157f0936810435a584099517`
was still running when these findings arrived. The coordinator authorized
stopping only the exact owned test because its inputs now needed to change.
The writer verified the framework interpreter, `-I -B`, harness, exact packet
selector, and owned parent/session; that process was observed at 4m56s, then
terminated by its single PID. It settled with exit 143 and is **inconclusive**,
not a pass and not evidence for the combined correction. No broad process kill
or live operation occurred.

| GitHub finding, in comment order | Disposition and regression evidence |
|---|---|
| 1: `setattr` sensitive names | Corrected the existing attribute-name guard to include direct/aliased `setattr` and qualified builtin forms. `test_pr103_gh_setattr_names` retains a literal-name positive. |
| 2: nested class launcher imports | Corrected class-suite traversal to include control-flow suites while stopping at nested method/class scope ownership. `test_pr103_gh_nested_class_launchers` covers `if`, loop, `try` and `with` imports; allowed `re.escape` remains accepted. |
| 3: destructuring mapping identity | Deterministic tuple/list target/source pairs now feed the existing scoped binding/alias graph. `test_pr103_gh_destructured_mapping_aliases` covers tuple, list and nested destructuring and retains literal keys. |
| 4: late-inserted conversion callables | The scoped mapping identity graph now also carries stored key/value entries from subscript stores, `update`, `setdefault`, `__setitem__` and union assignment. Subscript resolution selects these entries along with initial values. `test_pr103_gh_inserted_conversion_callables` covers all three numeric converters, aliases, numeric inputs and a known safe key. |
| 5: assigned display implementations | The existing display check resolves local function/lambda implementations assigned in a class suite or installed later on a class, including simple class aliases. `test_pr103_gh_assigned_display_methods` covers both installation forms with literal-return positives. |
| 6: bound/percent formatting | Display traversal expands bound `format`/`format_map` arguments, known formatter aliases and `%` operands. `test_pr103_gh_bound_and_percent_display` covers all three display protocols with safe display counterparts. |
| 7: unbound method binding | Duplicate of A. `test_pr103_delta_unbound_method_arguments` additionally includes the exact `C.lookup(C(), sensitive)` shape; this is resolved by the same class/instance binding correction, not a separate fix. |
| 8: implicit pathlib errors | Resolved local paths used in `relative_to` are refused unless a visible `ValueError` handler suppresses the exception without reading its exception binding. Raises must explicitly suppress context (`from None`) or call unshadowed builtin `SystemExit` without a cause; ordinary replacement exceptions and bare re-raises remain refused. `test_pr103_gh_implicit_path_errors` covers direct/aliased paths, a relative literal, suppression, re-raise and error output. Existing containment/reader controls remain selected. This correction does not claim every possible pathlib failure is modeled. |
| 9: helper-return launcher defaults | Existing launcher-default traversal follows local return values with its visited guard. `test_pr103_gh_helper_launcher_defaults` covers an OS launcher and a safe `len` factory. |
| 10: wrapped open readers | Open provenance follows selected literal containers, boolean/conditional values, aliases and local helper returns before existing path/mode checks apply. `test_pr103_gh_wrapped_open_readers` covers tuple/dictionary selection, a boolean expression and a factory, with safe `len` and known nonreader entries. A no-reader-source fast return avoids traversing return graphs when no open provenance can exist. |
| 11: class namespace environment imports | Class-suite exports of `os.environ`/`environb` are refused, including nested suites, rather than allowing unqualified seeds to lose their owning class. `test_pr103_gh_class_sensitive_imports` covers direct, class-alias and instance reads; `re.escape` remains accepted. |

Actual incremental results, using the identical isolated PATH/locale prefix
above (CPython 3.14.3):

- Prefix plus `-k test_pr103_gh` first reproduced 34 failures in ten methods,
  0.298s, exit 1, on the seven-class working correction. Several methods stopped
  at their first failed unsafe assertion; not all later subcases ran in RED.
  Three union-assignment converter cases were already rejected.
- First implementation run: ten methods, nine remaining failures, 0.378s.
  The existing general scope helper deliberately excludes class scopes;
  class-suite ownership was therefore handled explicitly instead of changing
  that helper's semantics. The same ten methods then passed in 0.437s.
- Expanded controls (explicit unbound receiver, `with` class import, caught and
  rethrown path errors, and mixed known-key reader containers) plus adjacent
  existing selectors passed 42 methods in 0.759s.
- Coordinator-requested adjacent probes reproduced two `update` source gaps
  (keyword entries and an aliased mapping) and a bound formatter alias gap:
  prefix plus `-k test_pr103_gh_adjacent` failed three assertions in two methods,
  0.217s. `update` now contributes keyword entries and mapping sources to the
  existing selection traversal; display traversal reuses the existing formatter
  alias resolver. The same two methods passed in 0.217s, including numeric and
  literal-display positives. These are extensions of findings 4/6, not new
  unrelated scope.

The combined final focused and packet results follow below. They supersede no

| Finding and source comment | Correction and bounded safe controls |
|---|---|
| Handler order, 6339148730 | The first potentially matching handler owns the proof. Unshadowed known nonmatching builtin exceptions may be skipped; unknown handler types fail closed, including unknown tuple elements. An earlier broad unsafe handler cannot be rescued by a later `ValueError: pass`. `test_pr103_review_handler_order` retains a nonmatching `TypeError` followed by safe suppression and a safe first handler followed by unreachable unsafe output. |
| Handler suppression, 6339148730 | Replace the absence-of-explicit-raise heuristic with a narrow positive proof: inert control flow, constant returns, or unshadowed builtin replacement exceptions with proven inert operands and explicit `from None` (or builtin `SystemExit`). Arbitrary expressions/calls, helper errors, operand-evaluation errors, unresolved names, nested `try/finally`, and noninert enclosing `finally` effects reject. `test_pr103_review_handler_suppression` retains `pass`, literal `from None`, and literal `SystemExit`. `test_pr103_review_handler_adjacent_proof` covers unknown tuple types, missing/late/unreachable/deleted names and an escaped CLI-argument alias. Existing packet `SystemExit` diagnostics retain acceptance through direct, preceding, same-suite primitive bindings: literal aliases or stable indexed CLI strings with no raw argument-list escape/store. This is a narrow proof, not arbitrary handler-code safety. |
| Starred mapping identity, 6339148730 | Literal destructuring binds prefix targets from the front and suffix targets from the tail; starred middle alternatives conservatively join the identity graph. Nested targets recurse through the same resolver. Unresolved source expansion retains all possible source identities instead of guessing a position. `test_pr103_review_starred_mapping_identity` covers prefix/tail, nested and starred-source shapes with literal-key positives. |
| Assigned display provenance, 6339148730 | Reuse `python_local_call_return_values` and the existing bounded subscript/container selector before inspecting assigned implementations. `test_pr103_review_assigned_display_provenance` covers direct/chained factory returns, tuple selection and named dictionaries for both class-suite and later installation, retaining literal-return counterparts. Shared visited guards preserve termination. |
| Lambda sensitive defaults, 6338442487 | Include lambdas in the existing fixed-point default-taint propagation and its iteration bound. `test_pr103_final_lambda_sensitive_defaults` covers positional and keyword-only defaults, with literal defaults accepted. |
| Open callable defaults, 6338442487 | Feed positional and keyword-only function/lambda defaults into the existing open-provenance assignment traversal. `test_pr103_final_open_callable_defaults` rejects private synthetic paths while preserving the same reviewed repository-relative reader and an unrelated `len` default. No reader/path allowlist was widened. |
| Breakpoint hook indirection, 6338442487 | Conservatively refuse breakpoint-hook attribute rebinding and direct unshadowed builtin `breakpoint` calls. Hook installation therefore rejects before a direct or aliased breakpoint call can hide it. `test_pr103_final_breakpoint_hook` retains an ordinary locally defined function named `breakpoint`; no debugger or hook specimen executes. |
| Custom iteration/sequence values, 6338442487 | Reuse the local class/assigned-method provenance graph for `__iter__`, `__next__` and `__getitem__`, inspecting return/yield expressions before collection/iterator consumption, including existing callable aliases. `test_pr103_final_custom_iterator_values` covers assigned lambdas, generator yields and sequence lookup with literal collection positives. `test_pr103_final_iterator_adjacent_provenance` retains safe self-iterator and assigned-generator alternatives. This conservatively rejects sensitive consumption without claiming a complete Python iterator model. |

### Preserve Markdown diagnostics outside active exception context

The writer added an actual-body regression rather than relaxing the scanner.
Prefix plus `-k test_pr103_current_markdown_diagnostics_suppress_context`
reproduced the exact canonical-body refusal in 0.878s, exit 1. Each of the two
Markdown containment checks now appends its diagnostic immediately before the
`try`, uses only `continue` in `except ValueError`, and pops the just-added
entry immediately after successful containment. The coordinator approved this
aggregation-preserving approach instead of the initially suggested fail-fast
alternative. No handler-call exemption, allowlist or reader-proof change was
made; both direct containment checks and diagnostic texts are retained.

This is sequential stack bookkeeping on the canonical builtin list and Path
flow. A successful check removes only the entry it just appended. A failed
check continues its own loop before the pop, retaining its diagnostic. Thus
prior errors, multiple failures and their order are preserved; successful
checks do not remove earlier errors. Fallible message construction and append
now occur before the path exception can become active. No runtime success or
exception behavior of a recipe was executed or fabricated.

-k test_pr103_review_handler_suppression` passed three methods in 0.768s,
exit 0. The combined focused command first passed 67 methods in 25.276s on
packet `025be857c0a0a7c83ab8cb32ae21a073312fb5b8` and harness
`1692b517745e245c8bfaae6364f03a39dbe78b95`; the same bounded compatibility
preflight passed in 17.085s, with 206 helpers/seven regex proofs/zero collisions.

For independent reproduction, the following consolidated trusted proof combines
the identity, ordered-state and corrected global-source audits above. It reads
the current trusted harness and packet, constructs only inert old-source bytes,
and invokes trusted static-check functions; it never executes a recipe.

```sh
env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B - <<'PY'
import ast
import hashlib
import runpy
import time
from pathlib import Path

started = time.monotonic()
h = runpy.run_path("scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py")
current = h["PACKET_TEXT"]
edits = (
    ('    errors.append(f"{name}: tracked Markdown path escapes repository")\n    try:\n        source_path.relative_to(repository_root)\n    except ValueError:\n        continue\n    errors.pop()',
     '    try:\n        source_path.relative_to(repository_root)\n    except ValueError:\n        errors.append(f"{name}: tracked Markdown path escapes repository")\n        continue'),
    ('        errors.append(f"{name}: local link target escapes repository {target}")\n        try:\n            path.relative_to(repository_root)\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        errors.pop()',
     '        try:\n            path.relative_to(repository_root)\n        except ValueError:\n            errors.append(f"{name}: local link target escapes repository {target}")\n            continue'),
)
old = current
for after, before in edits:
    assert old.count(after) == 1
    old = old.replace(after, before, 1)
def blob(value):
    data = value.encode()
    return hashlib.sha1(b"blob " + str(len(data)).encode() + b"\0" + data).hexdigest()
assert blob(old) == "08377ae9947f9623e2695f4866f8840b45dd9fcb"
now_harness = Path("scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py").read_text()
start = now_harness.index("    def test_pr103_current_markdown_diagnostics_suppress_context")
end = now_harness.index("    def test_pr103_current_scanner_method_helper_is_not_launcher_alias", start)
old_harness = now_harness[:start] + now_harness[end:]
assert blob(old_harness) == "f0bc9ac3543856e4f7e06fbd5d8333ccaff4b996"
assert h["_scanner_module_source"](old) == h["_scanner_module_source"](current)
prefix_end = now_harness.index("class Issue79RegressionTests(")
assert now_harness[:prefix_end] == old_harness[:prefix_end]
def selector(source):
    node = next(node for node in ast.walk(ast.parse(source))
                if isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef)
                and node.name == "test_current_packet_has_no_static_scanner_violations")
    return ast.get_source_segment(source, node)
assert selector(now_harness) == selector(old_harness)
a, b = h["_scanner_namespace"](), h["_scanner_namespace"]()
a["source"], b["source"] = old, current
state_names = ("shell_owned_path_variables", "shell_pending_owned_bindings",
               "shell_sensitive_variable_names")
def state(namespace):
    return tuple(frozenset(namespace[name]) for name in state_names)
def check(namespace, command):
    assert not namespace["shell_process_substitution"](command)
    for segment in namespace["shell_token_segments"](command):
        if namespace["python_stdin_command"](segment):
            assert namespace["reviewed_python_heredoc_segment"](command, segment)
        else:
            assert namespace["forbidden_shell_command"](segment) is None
count, offsets = 0, set()
for (left, left_line), (right, right_line) in zip(
        a["shell_commands"](old), b["shell_commands"](current), strict=True):
    count += 1
    assert left == right and state(a) == state(b)
    offsets.add(right_line - left_line)
    check(a, left)
    check(b, right)
    assert state(a) == state(b)
assert count == 261 and offsets == {0, 2}
final_state = state(b)
old_bodies = list(a["python_heredoc_bodies"](old))
new_bodies = list(b["python_heredoc_bodies"](current))
assert len(old_bodies) == len(new_bodies) == 86
changed = []
for index, (left, right) in enumerate(zip(old_bodies, new_bodies, strict=True)):
    assert left[2:] == right[2:]
    if left[1] != right[1]:
        changed.append(index)
assert changed == [14]
number, body, marker, invocation = new_bodies[14]
assert invocation["isolated"] and invocation["quoted"]
assert invocation["interpreter"] == "/opt/homebrew/bin/python3"
assert b["inspect_python_heredoc"](body, marker) is None
assert state(b) == final_state
module = ast.parse(h["_scanner_module_source"](current))
parents = {child: parent for parent in ast.walk(module)
           for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(parent)}
for node in ast.walk(module):
    if not isinstance(node, ast.Name) or node.id != "source" or not isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load):
        continue
    b["python_sensitive_imported_function_alias"](node, module, parents)
    index = getattr(module, "_issue79_sensitive_imported_alias_bindings", {})
    for scope in b["python_sensitive_alias_scope_chain"](node, parents):
        assert not isinstance(scope, ast.Module), "global packet-source read"
        bindings = index.get((id(scope), "source"), ())
        assert not any(kind == "global" for kind, _ in bindings)
        if bindings:
            break
mutators = {"shell_commands", "shell_record_sensitive_assignments", "shell_owned_directory_proof"}
references = []
for function in module.body:
    if isinstance(function, ast.FunctionDef):
        for node in ast.walk(function):
            if isinstance(node, ast.Name) and isinstance(node.ctx, ast.Load) and node.id in mutators:
                references.append((function.name, node.id))
assert sorted(references) == [
    ("shell_commands", "shell_owned_directory_proof"),
    ("shell_commands", "shell_record_sensitive_assignments"),
]
print("Old packet/harness:", blob(old), blob(old_harness))
print("Current packet/harness:", blob(current), blob(now_harness))
print("Final shell states:", {name: sorted(b[name]) for name in state_names})
print(f"PASS composed coverage proof: 261 streaming shell checks; 85 identical bodies reused; "
      f"changed body {changed[0]} at line {number} PASS; exact identity/context checks; "
      f"{time.monotonic() - started:.3f}s")

PY
```

## Nine P1 corrections from exact head `91e0d30`

The sole writer verified clean starting head
`91e0d30aee73b2aa6ea86bba529d619ed8768c38` and read the entire immutable
[GitHub finding comment 6340344117](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6340344117)
with `gh api repos/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/issues/comments/6340344117 --jq .body`.
All nine findings are blocking P1; none is waived, deferred or rebutted.
Authorship follows the explicit Codex `gpt-6.1-sol`, medium override.
This section records local writer evidence, not independent or GitHub approval.

The trusted harness loader, validators, selected methods and scanner changes
were inspected before loading them. Every reported witness, including the
destructive-looking launcher string, remained inert AST data. No specimen,
recipe, returned callable, debugger, encoder or custom protocol executed.
Only inspected trusted scanner/harness definitions ran, using the PATH/locale
environment and CPython 3.14.3 isolated invocation below. No GitHub write,
push, merge, live/canary operation or Docker operation ran; no credential
values were inspected or output.

| Finding in immutable comment order | Correction and focused controls |
|---|---|
| Custom iterators consumed by comprehensions | Apply the existing local protocol-return check to comprehension and ordinary loop iterables, using the same `__iter__`/`__next__`/`__getitem__` provenance as constructors. `test_pr103_nine_comprehension_protocols` covers list/set/generator/dictionary comprehensions with sensitive and literal sources. |
| Display methods installed through `setattr` | Feed direct and aliased builtin setters with literal protocol names into the existing class-method index. `test_pr103_nine_setter_display` covers the three display protocols and the adjacent iterator protocol with safe-return counterparts. |
| Open provenance through comprehensions | Resolve yielded callable values and their scoped comprehension bindings, retaining the existing reader/path checks. `test_pr103_nine_open_comprehensions` rejects the exact private-path form and dictionary extraction, while the same reviewed repository reader and `len` remain accepted. Ordinary loop-held readers use the same graph. |
| Bound `relative_to` aliases | Resolve callable values before applying the existing local-path/handler proof. `test_pr103_nine_bound_relative_to` covers direct, container and helper-return aliases; literal relative paths and existing safe `ValueError` suppression remain accepted. |
| Dunder attribute operations | Inspect the sensitive name for direct/aliased `__getattribute__`, `__setattr__` and `__delattr__`. Unknown bound/unbound descriptor shapes conservatively retain both first operands; bound calls therefore cover argument zero. Direct, alias, bound and literal-name controls are retained. |
| Helper-returned launchers assigned to attributes | Resolve local returns and selected container alternatives when recording class/instance attribute assignments, including class-suite assignment. `test_pr103_nine_returned_attribute_launchers` rejects all three forms with `os.system` and accepts the identical `len` alternatives. |
| Breakpoint aliases | Resolve builtin identity through the shared value graph before the existing debugger rejection. `test_pr103_nine_breakpoint_aliases` covers direct aliases, containers, defaults and loop targets; a local function shadowing `breakpoint` remains accepted. |
| Reversible encoder taint | Propagate sensitive arguments at imported module/callable boundaries, including direct imports, callable aliases and module receiver aliases. `test_pr103_nine_encoder_taint` and the adjacent alias method cover `quote`, `quote_plus`, `shlex.quote` and chained encoding, literal inputs and lexical local shadowing. No encoder-specific allowlist is added. |
| Sensitive `format_map` fields | Resolve direct/bound formatter targets and reject a sensitive receiver that may become a missing-field diagnostic. `test_pr103_nine_format_map_fields` covers direct/aliased calls and the exact reversible-hex witness, retaining literal-field controls. |

`python_value_provenance` is a finite worklist over expression/selection pairs.
It reuses the complete scoped binder inventory, follows assignment/default/
loop/comprehension/local-return alternatives, and caches only on the current
AST. Known single literal container keys are selected precisely. Nested
extraction conservatively flattens reachable container values, bounding cycles
without an ever-growing index stack; it does not promise arbitrary nested-key
precision. `test_pr103_nine_adjacent_cycles` and the nested/loop method retain
safe alternatives and verify termination. Unknown binders stop lexical lookup.
The imported-callable predicate reuses the same graph and scoped inventory.

The new argument propagation applies to imported module callables. Ordinary
methods retain their existing receiver policy: a safe dictionary lookup does
not copy its sensitive lookup key into the returned value. The experimental
join early-false return and subprocess environment/expanded-keyword exemption
were removed before final validation. The join branch is byte-identical to
the starting source, and there is no new process-consumer exemption or policy
allowlist. Existing local-format return proofs and all reader policy remain.

```sh
env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B - <<'PY'
import ast, runpy, subprocess, time
started=time.monotonic()
h=runpy.run_path('scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py'); s=h['_scanner_namespace']()
old_source=subprocess.run(['/usr/bin/git','--no-replace-objects','-P','-c','core.fsmonitor=false','-c','core.hooksPath=/dev/null','show','34968872cd1d1f832c9ee02dcf2119badc18bacc:docs/evidence/g01-recovery-packet.md'],env={'PATH':'/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin','LC_ALL':'C','GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM':'1','GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL':'/dev/null','GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM':'/dev/null','GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM':'1'},check=True,stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.PIPE,text=True).stdout
old=ast.parse(h['_scanner_module_source'](old_source)); current=ast.parse(h['_scanner_module_source'](h['PACKET_TEXT']))
a=s['python_import_bindings'](old)[1]; b=s['python_import_bindings'](current)[1]
helpers={n.name for n in old.body if isinstance(n,ast.FunctionDef)}
called={n.func.id for n in ast.walk(current) if isinstance(n,ast.Call) and isinstance(n.func,ast.Name)}
compared=helpers & called
collisions=sorted(name for name in compared if name in b and name not in a)
def proofs(tree):
    parents={c:p for p in ast.walk(tree) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(p)}
    result=set()
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not (isinstance(node,ast.Call) and isinstance(node.func,ast.Attribute) and node.func.attr=='group' and s['python_known_non_path_reader_call'](node,tree)):
            continue
        owner=node
        while owner in parents and not isinstance(owner,ast.FunctionDef): owner=parents[owner]
        result.add((owner.name if isinstance(owner,ast.FunctionDef) else None,ast.dump(node.func.value,include_attributes=False)))
    return result
prior=proofs(old); now=proofs(current)
assert len(compared)==206,len(compared)
assert len(prior)==7,(len(prior),prior)
assert not collisions,collisions
assert prior <= now,prior-now
print(f'PASS aggregate preflight: {len(compared)} called baseline helpers; {len(prior)} retained scoped regex-receiver proofs; zero new collisions/lost proofs; {time.monotonic()-started:.3f}s')
PY

| Confirmed blocking family | Shared correction and controls |
|---|---|
| Chained multi-target assignment | Reuse the complete inventory's unknown-Name binder, recognizing direct targets of the same `Assign` and following its common RHS. No inventory or unknown-binder policy is weakened. Chained breakpoint, setter-display and encoder aliases retain `len`/literal controls. |
| Class-member provenance | The same worklist resolves class identities through scoped class bindings, aliases, instances and inherited classes, then retains class-suite and later member assignments. Context-dependent recursive queries share the visited state and do not populate the root-query cache. Class breakpoint/encoder/path aliases retain safe counterparts; cyclic class members terminate. The writer's adjacent `launch = C.go` hypothesis was reproduced and fixed by resolving the final call's provenance before matching recorded launcher attributes. |

A starred middle is represented as inert list AST data, retaining its later subscript selection. Known safe positions are not mixed with other row entries. Scoped row aliases, cyclic alternatives, loop/comprehension readers, nested/starred targets and breakpoint targets are covered. Resolved callable identity now feeds both existing readable-open and open-mutation checks, including direct extracted calls. No path/mode policy is widened. |
| Unbound `str.format_map` | A resolved unbound builtin `str` descriptor uses the first call argument as its format string; bound receivers retain the prior check. Direct calls, callable aliases and a `str` class alias retain literal-field controls. |

### V2 HOLD: call-target expressions and supplied parameters

Renewed independent contract/security review settled HOLD on packet
`17ed29f05d99d294511c98dd5362c944dfddbe8e` and harness
`bdb6a480516a950474983efbaa891033a586bb77`. The two blocking families are

read completely with `gh api repos/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/issues/comments/6341046534
--jq .body`. Contract reported ten inert probes in 0.337s; security reported
27 in 0.339s and 0.434s. The writer again held source until both readers
settled and the coordinator released the barrier. No full packet invocation
ran on V2, and its 84 passes remain writer evidence without review approval.

The generic imported-callable argument branch now considers every AST call
target expression, including direct subscript extraction. Attribute receiver
handling remains specific to attributes. There is no list/dictionary encoder
spelling rule. Supplied values now use a cached AST-parameter source index
which reuses the existing function/lambda/method candidate and bound/static
method helpers. It records argument/selection edges before the value worklist
resolves them, avoiding recursive index construction. Positional, named,

The first exact unsafe body supplies `pack = (D,) if False else (*other,)`,
`other = (C,)`, `C.step = breakpoint`, `D.step = len`, to
`invoke("safe", *pack)`, whose `targets[0].step()` was accepted. A precise
plain expansion had discarded the nested-starred alternative. The shared
sequence worklist now recursively projects nested single-spread packs before
selecting their position. Every returned alternative is retained; an unresolved
or cyclic spread remains collection data beside precise alternatives. Flattening
has its own finite mode, and destructured projection no longer filters all
other alternatives merely because one literal alternative exists. Known safe
selected positions stay precise. Cyclic packs are covered without evaluating
their Python semantics.

The second exact unsafe body calls `apply(E)` where
`apply(cls): cls.invoke(None, C)` and `E.invoke(self, target): target.step()`.

to be an instance. The existing value graph now has a receiver-origin query
mode which retains constructor calls separately from class values through
aliases, selections and actual-argument edges. Constructor identities do not
become class identities in that mode. The staged binder publishes explicit
and implicit receiver offsets only for the corresponding proven origins;
genuinely mixed class/instance alternatives retain both. Unresolved receivers
wait for later stages rather than permanently publishing a guessed offset.
Static method/decorator and existing fallback alias policies are unchanged.
The finite binding key now includes the bound/unbound bit.

env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B - <<'PY'
import hashlib, runpy, sys, unittest

assert blob=='9ba77b69a26403e51195aaab7019f02540d23249',blob
h['_scanner_namespace'].__globals__['PACKET_TEXT']=original

result=unittest.TextTestRunner(verbosity=2).run(suite)
print('source-matched RED packet:',blob)
sys.exit(not result.wasSuccessful())
PY
```

helper used `pattern, source_value = pending_pairs.pop()`. The unchanged

A bounded extension over the same baseline AST found **328 called original
helper names including nested definitions**, with exactly one newly introduced
command-name collision: `source_value`. Two tiny inert contrasts reproduced
the nested-call refusal and acceptance after changing only the unrelated
destructured local spelling to `projection_source_value`; inventory and both
contrasts took **6.490s** including loader. Both readers acknowledged paused
no-read barriers (`msg_67c893223d95`, `msg_b17b0fee8ff5`) before the coordinator
authorized the rename and durable expanded preflight.

The new regression derives the actual projection-helper AST as inert source
beside the baseline-shaped nested callable, and preserves a separate refusal
control for the original unresolved-destructuring shape. Prefix plus
`-k test_pr103_projection_bookkeeping_preserves_nested_helper_calls` failed
one assertion in **0.412s**, exit 1, on packet
`136ecdacda22736a34d0ea5a566f2d58d6cd0bb5` and RED harness
`51a5f50d01253909e7a61b72dbc0014ef6fbd66d`. Renaming only the new projection
local and its references to `projection_source_value` passed the same method
in **0.574s**, exit 0. No classifier, allowlist, existing helper or scope

The unchanged combined focused command passed **100 methods in 26.421s**,
exit 0, retaining all prior 99 plus the new regression and actual canonical
body/containment checks. The expanded aggregate preflight passed in
**14.303s**, exit 0: 206 root helpers retained, 328 called original helper
names including nested definitions checked, seven retained scoped regex
proofs, zero new collisions or lost proofs. Its exact durable command is:

```sh
env -i PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin LC_ALL=C /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.14 -I -B - <<'PY'
import ast, runpy, subprocess, time
started=time.monotonic()
h=runpy.run_path('scripts/evidence_packet/issue79_regression_test.py'); s=h['_scanner_namespace']()
old_source=subprocess.run(['/usr/bin/git','--no-replace-objects','-P','-c','core.fsmonitor=false','-c','core.hooksPath=/dev/null','show','34968872cd1d1f832c9ee02dcf2119badc18bacc:docs/evidence/g01-recovery-packet.md'],env={'PATH':'/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/bin:/bin','LC_ALL':'C','GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM':'1','GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL':'/dev/null','GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM':'/dev/null','GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM':'1'},check=True,stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.PIPE,text=True).stdout
old=ast.parse(h['_scanner_module_source'](old_source)); current=ast.parse(h['_scanner_module_source'](h['PACKET_TEXT']))
a=s['python_import_bindings'](old)[1]; b=s['python_import_bindings'](current)[1]
root_helpers={n.name for n in old.body if isinstance(n,ast.FunctionDef)}
all_helpers={n.name for n in ast.walk(old) if isinstance(n,(ast.FunctionDef,ast.AsyncFunctionDef))}
called={n.func.id for n in ast.walk(current) if isinstance(n,ast.Call) and isinstance(n.func,ast.Name)}
compared=root_helpers & called; all_compared=all_helpers & called
collisions=sorted(name for name in all_compared if name in b and name not in a)
def proofs(tree):
    parents={c:p for p in ast.walk(tree) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(p)}
    result=set()
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not (isinstance(node,ast.Call) and isinstance(node.func,ast.Attribute) and node.func.attr=='group' and s['python_known_non_path_reader_call'](node,tree)):
            continue
        owner=node
        while owner in parents and not isinstance(owner,ast.FunctionDef): owner=parents[owner]
        result.add((owner.name if isinstance(owner,ast.FunctionDef) else None,ast.dump(node.func.value,include_attributes=False)))
    return result
prior=proofs(old); now=proofs(current)
assert len(compared)==206,len(compared)
assert len(all_compared)==328,len(all_compared)
assert len(prior)==7,(len(prior),prior)
assert not collisions,collisions
assert prior <= now,prior-now
print(f'PASS expanded aggregate preflight: {len(compared)} root helpers; {len(all_compared)} called original helpers including nested; {len(prior)} retained scoped regex-receiver proofs; zero new collisions/lost proofs; {time.monotonic()-started:.3f}s')
PY
```

The finite valid awaitable specimen is recorded here as **inert AST text only**:

```python
import os
class Awaitable:
    def __await__(self):
        if False:
            yield None
        return os.environ
class L:
    def __anext__(self):
        return Awaitable()
class K:
    def __aiter__(self):
        return L()
async def collect():
    async for item in K():
        print(item)
        break
collect().send(None)
```

The unreachable yield makes `__await__` a generator returning the environment
as its completion value. A plain ordinary `def __await__: return os.environ`
does not supply a valid await iterator and is **not** claimed as a demonstrated
runtime leak. The class-mutation specimen uses the same finite consumer,
ordinary compatible heap classes, `L.async __anext__` returning the environment,
and `K.__aiter__` calling `object.__setattr__(self, "__class__", L)` before
returning `self`. These are semantic observations about AST text; neither
specimen was run and no runtime output observation is claimed.

### V13 disclosed unresolved factory-shaped nonlocal acceptance

Coordinator `msg_62e60092ce61` requested the complete source and exact identities
for the extra acceptance disclosed in `msg_7396d71442c9`. After the focused
and preflight runs completed, a read-only two-namespace authoritative probe
returned **`None` on both** untouched baseline packet `cd8c5807d676814e2ba86a3b3f6a85b2bd4b4137`
at `84e1f11cf08b3a1914c9446a00511e7739297c01` and current frozen packet
`765c0a77b2e52f176780d6bf28fb9f4393eeb2cb`, using final harness
`08aca2cdf850ac59997432d2757691b16396c1b4` (command exit 0). The source was
passed only as a string to `inspect_python_heredoc(source, False)`; neither
the factory nor the protocol or consumer executed:

```python
import os
class L:
    async def __anext__(self):
        return os.environ
def build():
    saved = None
    def mutate():
        object.__setattr__(saved, "__class__", L)
    class K:
        def __aiter__(self):
            nonlocal saved
            saved = self
            mutate()
            return self
        async def __anext__(self):
            return "safe"
    return K
K = build()
async def collect():
    async for item in K():
        print(item)
        break
collect().send(None)
```

By Python semantics this is a complete finite sensitive path: `saved` is
declared in the enclosing function, `nonlocal saved` binds the actual receiver
there, `mutate` reaches that same cell, and the ordinary compatible heap classes
permit changing the receiver to `L` before async-next dispatch. This statement
is semantic reasoning, not an observed runtime leak. The current correction
does **not** close this acceptance. The coordinator requested that reviewers
assess a bounded refusal at the existing return/class boundary before deciding
whether architecture expansion is needed. No wider sweep, factory engine,
source edit, full scan or commit follows this disclosure without a new gate;
source remains frozen at the identities above.

### V22 phase 1 — proposed bounded selected-value proof, awaiting design review

The current task explicitly authorizes the formerly blocked expansion:
order-sensitive mapping overwrite, finite key domains and alias/mutation/call
escape invalidation. Authorship remains the explicit Codex `gpt-6.1-sol`, medium
override. This proposal supersedes the earlier authorization limit, not the
earlier failed results or HOLD verdicts. Two independent design reviews and
coordinator disposition are required before packet/harness edits. No new test
result or implemented behavior is claimed in this phase.

Read contract: issue 79 acceptance requires the smallest fail-closed correction,
preserved safe positives and evidence reuse, focused RED/GREEN, exact commands,
review URLs and rollback evidence. AGENTS.md, docs/EXECUTION.md,
docs/SECURITY-DESIGN.md and the local continuation remain applicable; this is
scanner analysis of inert AST, not authorization to run recipes or live code.
Input HEAD is `66c33aa19ab72603e48a8deb67fbe7d11064da6f`; inspected input packet,
harness and ledger blobs are respectively
`e5e30bdd659102b73584c28fdbf60db2084f5aa7`,
`97c9271e7fb93bf45fd82249c2b02976ee20bedb` and
`bdb9ebf6754fd7098e4fa6a5b46a5b3ab62f57a6`.

**Concrete conflict.** The selected-store union at
`error_operand_subscript_values` retains all historical env stores across
`go_env = dict(env)`. The canonical status print selects CGO_ENABLED,
GOEXPERIMENT, GOFIPS140 and GOWORK. Each has a definite literal/literal-bound
pin before the copy, after the command-prefix loop. The unrelated later
`os.environ.get(name)` loop must not become the earlier command-prefix
`value` binding. The finite private-module `.items()` loop writes only its
three actual dictionary keys; its `reviewed` binding cannot stand for every
environment key. Subsequent GOFLAGS assignment is to another key. However,
go_env also reaches run_go_child, run_cgo_version_child and Popen before the
print. Discarding those edges without an effects proof would be unsound.

**Boundary and state.** Add a dedicated selected-mapping proof adjacent to the
existing selected-store/output check; do not replace general provenance,
global sensitive-name taint or local-class protocol analysis. Its result is
SAFE, SENSITIVE or UNKNOWN with nonempty provenance, never a truthy empty
list. SAFE may discharge only the newly added stored-value output edge for
that exact selection. SENSITIVE and UNKNOWN refuse there; an unrelated
existing security gate cannot be discharged. Use lexical binding identities,
allocation/copy identities, program points, per-key value alternatives, a
wildcard-key alternative and persistent escape status. Copy freezes the
source state at the copy point into a distinct identity; ordinary assignment
shares identity. Querying a stored scalar captures its value at the store
point, rather than re-reading all later bindings of the same spelling.

**Small supported syntax and kills.** Support plain string/number/None
constants; direct lexical names; literal dictionaries; exact unshadowed
builtin dict positional/keyword copies and dictionary unpack; direct alias
assignment; subscript stores; literal/closed-map update and union stores;
and conditional expression unions with every alternative retained. A safe
scalar needs closed literal provenance, not merely absence from global taint.
Unresolved expressions are UNKNOWN. Definite stores to one proven exact
builtin-string key kill older alternatives for that key only. Assignments
under if/try/match/while/for, short-circuit or comprehension ancestry are weak
updates and never kill an earlier sensitive/unknown alternative. No branch
execution, arbitrary condition evaluation or general Python interpreter is
introduced. A later unconditional pin may kill prior weak stores, but not
an escaped identity. setdefault is a weak update unless absence is proved;
unsupported mutation/delete becomes UNKNOWN for affected keys, or wildcard
when its domain is unknown. Rebinding creates a new binding version; old
aliases continue to reference the old allocation. Function/comprehension
binders with the same spelling do not pollute module bindings.

**Finite domains and closure.** For literal string-key dictionaries and
literal string sets/tuples/lists, resolve the complete domain at the use point
through supported unshadowed aliases/copies only. A direct `.items()` loop
over such a proven builtin dictionary binds key/value pairs together, with
the corresponding literal value alternatives, not an uncorrelated global
name. Such loops remain weak updates: zero iterations, break/continue,
exceptions or body control cannot establish a pin. A domain may exclude a
selected key only when every possible key is known and unequal to it; a
single unknown spread/key/conditional arm retains wildcard. Inventory all
aliases, rebinding and mutations of the domain provider before use; later
mutations inside the iterated body, reachable callbacks, or escapes make its
domain UNKNOWN. No key spelling, source path, helper name or fixture text is
a safety certificate. Initial inherited environment/dynamic-prefix values
may remain UNKNOWN; their analysis need not become a new interpreter because
the later definite per-key pins dominate them.

**Alias/call closure.** Build scoped alias/allocation reachability before
using a SAFE proof. Track direct aliases and copies separately. Container or
attribute storage, return/yield, default capture, closure/callback capture,
unknown starred arguments, and unresolved alias or callable alternatives
invalidate when they can expose the mapping/domain dependency. Unknown call
escape is persistent; a later pin cannot erase a retained alias. Scan nested
function bodies for capture, but distinguish definition from invocation;
unknown invocation of a capturing callable invalidates. A nested alias store
cannot disappear merely because its name belongs to another lexical scope.
Recursive/cyclic or over-budget proof queries yield UNKNOWN.

Proposed hard bounds are 256 alternatives per key/domain, 32 local-call edges
on one proof path and 65,536 distinct proof-state visits per selected query;
exceeding any bound yields UNKNOWN and refusal, with no truncation accepted
as complete. Empty value provenance is UNKNOWN; an explicitly proved empty
builtin dictionary has an empty key domain but cannot prove a selected value
exists. Mutable stored values remain references: later mutation of a list or
mapping stored at x invalidates a literal snapshot proof, even after a shallow
dict copy. Only primitive literal values are scalar snapshots.

Calls touching the identity require one of two explicit proofs: a small
builtin-dict read/copy operation on a proven exact builtin receiver with
closed primitive keys, or a bounded local-function effect summary derived
from its body and actual arguments. The summary follows positional/keyword
parameters, **kwargs forwarding and captured identities, checks all branches,
and reports no-mutate/no-retain only if every mapping use and transitive
callee is supported. It must detect stores, callbacks, replacement of a
callee/module attribute and unknown alternatives; no helper-name exemption.
Calls with no reachable mapping, alias or capturing callback do not create
an edge merely because they occur between pin and print. Dynamic namespace
access and unresolved reflective access cannot prove closure.

The only external mapping consumer needed by the canonical path is the
already classified subprocess creation primitive's `env` argument. Its
no-mutate/no-retain summary is limited to a stable, unshadowed subprocess
binding, exact builtin mapping and primitive contents, with existing launcher
and override gates still required. Forwarded kwargs must resolve `env` to
that argument, not arbitrary expansion. A local wrapper qualifies only by
the preceding structural summary. Unknown callbacks, shadowed Popen, patched
subprocess, dynamic env payloads or unsupported forwarding refuse. The
implementation must demonstrate this summary on actual canonical call paths;
if it cannot, report the remaining proof gap before relaxing any rule. This
is an explicit primitive semantic boundary for reviewer assessment, not a
claim that Python mapping calls are generally pure.

Coordinator CPython source inspection (`msg_c4424fa35c6a`) identifies an
additional explicit precondition: POSIX subprocess creation exposes env to
`sys.audit` before spawn, and converts env/other arguments. Consequently the
primitive summary requires a trusted standard interpreter/stdlib baseline
with no ambient hook that mutates or retains the supplied mapping. This is
an analysis assumption, not hostile-interpreter isolation or evidence that
`-I` removes hooks. Specimen hook registration (`sys.addaudithook`, including
aliases), replacement of sys.audit, runtime callback registration such as
os.register_at_fork, and unresolved reflective access to those bindings
invalidate the primitive summary; none can be dismissed because the hook
was defined before go_env or receives the mapping indirectly via audit args.
Registration of an unknown callback cannot prove a no-effect summary.

All subprocess arguments that can execute a conversion must also be closed:
argv is an exact builtin list/tuple of primitive strings/bytes, env an exact
builtin dictionary of primitive strings/bytes, and cwd/executable absent,
primitive strings/bytes, or exact stable standard pathlib path objects with
unmodified constructor/conversion methods. No custom iterable, path-like,
string subclass or custom mapping can qualify. Explicit preexec_fn and other
process-creation overrides remain refused by the existing launcher gates;
an opaque forwarded option does not become safe through this summary. Local
wrapper kwargs are a separate fresh dictionary containing env by reference;
its pop/store operations must be shown to affect that outer dictionary only,
and all callback/default/override alternatives must close. Stability includes
the referenced subprocess, sys, os and pathlib bindings and attributes,
including their aliases; unresolved module/attribute mutation is UNKNOWN.
The canonical helper's argv/cwd/type and no-hook obligations must be checked
on actual syntax before implementation can claim this primitive summary.

The primitive-contents obligation cannot be inferred from safe selected x
alone. Limited type facts may establish exact string keys/values from stable
os.environ `.items()` and sys.argv string elements, their builtin list copies,
string `.split()` destructuring and literal string replacements. These facts
must close source/module binding mutation, all intervening writes and helper
effects, retain unknown/custom-key alternatives, and confer no nonsensitivity
on environment values. No custom `__hash__`, `__eq__`, `__str__` or path-like
callback may be hidden by that type fact. If these limited facts do not close
the actual canonical environment, the primitive summary remains UNKNOWN;
report that gap instead of extending the language subset silently.

**Concrete RED/controls after design approval.** Preserve the existing
canonical-positive RED and scope-collision RED; add inert specimens before
the fix (planned expectations below, not observed results):

| Boundary | Accept control | Refuse control |
|---|---|---|
| Dominant pin/copy | `m={"x":"old"}; m["x"]=os.environ; m["x"]="safe"; c=dict(m); print(c["x"])` | Reverse the last two writes; or copy before the pin and print the old copy. |
| Conditional/loop pins | Unconditional pin after an uncertain store | Put only the safe pin under `if flag`, `while flag` or `for _ in unknown`; the prior sensitive value survives. |
| Finite items domain | After a safe x pin, store sensitive values only through `.items()` of `{"other":os.environ}` | Include x, conditional x/other, unknown key/spread, or mutate/escape the domain provider before iteration. |
| Store-point binding | Safe module value stored before a later sensitive rebind; same-name unrelated function | Sensitive value at the store, mixed safe/sensitive conditional value, unknown value or nested nonlocal/global mutation. |
| Alias and copy | Copy made after safe pin; harmless/different-key later alias writes | Later alias x store, alias captured in container/default/callback, alias rebind retaining old allocation, or unknown call after pin. |
| Mutable selected value | Primitive literal snapshot independent of later rebinding | Store a list at x, shallow-copy the dictionary, then append os.environ through the old list alias before printing the copy. |
| Call proof | Direct local read-only wrapper and stable primitive env forwarding | Add a wrapper store/retain/callback, change Popen binding, unknown callee branch, **kwargs ambiguity, or call an escaped callback after a later pin. |
| Constructors | Exact unshadowed dict with safe selected value | Shadow/rebind dict or its alias; one safe constructor alternative must not hide an unknown one. |
| Existing domains | Original environment keys-only positives; safe selected values and safe-key-only stores | Original inherited mapping leaks, sensitive key representations and local-ClassDef protocol/mutation refusals. |

Also test try/except partial writes, callback default versus call-site override,
global/nonlocal helper writes, custom hash/equality keys and the configured
proof limits. These are deliberate unsupported/refusal cases where closed
proof is unavailable, even when one spelling happens to return a safe value.
Primitive-specific negative controls register an audit hook which stores or
retains the env found in its args; replace sys.audit; register an at-fork
callback; and supply custom argv iterable/cwd path-like conversion callbacks.
Pair these with the stable primitive/literal-argument control and the actual
canonical path. They remain inert source strings, never hook registration or
process execution in the test runtime.

Use one approved implementation batch: meaningful RED, minimal GREEN, then
combined focused and expanded V9 preflight once, with exact receipts including
failed/inconclusive outcomes. Check diff and added-secret/path hygiene, freeze
packet/harness identities, and request two independent source delta reviews.
No source/harness edits, new tests, full-packet qualification, commit, push or
GitHub writes occurred in phase 1. Full qualification remains reserved for the
stable exact-head GitHub-approved candidate under coordinator control.

#### V22 normative design addendum — complete effect grammar and canonical trace

This addendum incorporates coordinator messages `msg_cd5f2ec770c3` and
`msg_f4da7b789170` and is normative where the preceding proposal is less
specific. It remains design only. Applicability is structural reachability
from an output selection through mapping bindings/copies/aliases to explicit
recorded mapping stores; unrelated subscripts retain existing gates. Unknown
alias/store alternatives on a relevant graph cannot remove that obligation.

The proof is a finite dependency/effect analysis, not an interpreter for
surrounding Python. It traverses dependency-free syntax for hidden captures
and mutations but does not evaluate it. The following is the closed transfer
grammar; any dependency-bearing form outside it produces UNKNOWN:

| Form | Supported transfer/effect |
|---|---|
| Constant, Name | Primitive constant or lexical binding at the actual use point; global/nonlocal loads in a local summary resolve at invocation, not function definition. Future rebinding of a captured global must affect the invocation. |
| Dict, List, Tuple, Set | Exact builtin allocation with all entries/elements closed; dictionary unpack follows complete source alternatives. Lists/tuples may carry references; these are never scalar snapshots. Unknown/custom keys retain protocol effects and wildcard domain. |
| Subscript, Attribute | Exact builtin selection by a primitive key/index; supported stable module API or exact standard-path member only. Unknown descriptors, properties and reflective lookups are UNKNOWN. |
| Assign, AnnAssign, NamedExpr | Versioned name binding or exact builtin subscript store. Destructuring only from fixed literal pairs, proven `.items()` pairs, argv/string split results. NamedExpr under conditional ancestry cannot establish a strong pin. Attribute/unknown-target stores are escapes or mutation invalidation. |
| AugAssign | Builtin-dict union of complete mapping alternatives only. Other dependency-bearing operators are UNKNOWN. |
| IfExp, BoolOp, Compare | Retain all value/effect alternatives. No truth-value evaluation or arbitrary predicate refinement. Comparisons involving custom operands are UNKNOWN. |
| BinOp, JoinedStr | Primitive string concatenation; exact stable pathlib path joining with closed operands; primitive-only formatting. These establish type/effect facts, not nonsensitivity of unknown contents. |
| If, For, While, Try/TryStar, Match, With | Traverse every body/handler/finally/alternative for dependency effects. No inner pin kills incoming state. Only finite literal/closed-items iteration supplies key/value domains; other iteration is UNKNOWN when relevant. Unknown context-manager or pattern protocol effects invalidate. |
| FunctionDef, Lambda | Resolve defaults at definition and captured free bindings at invocation; retained defaults/callbacks invalidate escaped identities. A unique stable local callable may receive the bounded structural effect summary. Async, yield, recursive or indirect unknown invocation is UNKNOWN when relevant. |
| Return, Raise, Expr | Follow dependency-bearing operands; returning/raising/retaining the mapping or a holder/callback reaching it is escape. Primitive returns do not escape the mapping. |
| Calls on exact builtin mappings | get/items/keys/values read only with closed primitive key/default operands; dict copy/unpack freezes a shallow snapshot. update/subscript/union mutate per complete domain; setdefault is weak; pop removes only its closed key and returns that entry reference. Unknown method/callable binding invalidates. |
| Calls on exact builtin sequences/strings | list/tuple copy, slicing, append/extend/pop with closed contents; string split/strip and primitive string concatenation/formatting. Container mutation updates alias-reachable types/references; no custom iterator or conversion is assumed pure. |
| Local calls | Bind positional/default/keyword arguments and a fresh outer **kwargs allocation; preserve every nested reference. Traverse effects of the body, all defaults/overrides/callbacks and captured globals. A closed summary may separate fresh outer kwargs mutation from retained env allocation. Unknown expansion or callable alternative is UNKNOWN. |
| Stable stdlib type sources | os.environ string pairs; sys.argv string elements; stable pathlib Path construction/cwd/resolve/absolute/join; shutil.which primitive string-or-None; tempfile temporary-directory name and returned standard Path values through inspected local returns. Each is conditional on stable imported/module/class/instance bindings, primitive inputs and no callback escape; these facts describe types/effects only. |
| Stable subprocess creation | The env-only no-mutate/no-retain contract below, after all argument/hook obligations close; no general external-call purity. |

Bindings used in any row must be stable across aliases, `setattr`/`delattr`,
subscript namespace access, import/constructor rebinding and reachable helper
effects. Unknown reflective write invalidates relevant callable/module/type
facts. Patching Path/PosixPath conversion methods or an instance conversion
invalidates a standard-path fact, including a patch made through an alias or
helper. Mutation of os.environ/sys.argv or their module bindings invalidates
their type-source summary unless all resulting alternatives remain proven
exact primitive contents. Mixed known/unknown constructor choices are UNKNOWN.
Bounds remain 256 alternatives, 32 call edges and 65,536 state visits; proof
limits never silently drop an alternative.

**Trusted runtime boundary.** The scoped external contract is specifically
CPython 3.14.3 POSIX subprocess creation with trusted, stable standard-library
implementation, no ambient mutating/retaining audit or fork hooks, and no
concurrent hostile interpreter mutation. For a closed builtin env mapping
and callback-free arguments, the primitive reads/converts env without
mutating it or retaining its allocation beyond construction. This says
nothing about retaining argv: Popen stores args, and process/result objects
may retain that separate sequence. It does not make arbitrary subprocess
calls pure or authorize them. `None` or another inert primitive causing an
argument error is not a successful-execution claim; unknown custom conversions
cannot receive the contract. An env reference in argv, cwd, a process option,
callback or another retained holder invalidates the env-only summary.

Specimen audit/fork hook registration, whether direct, aliased, indirect or
inside a helper, invalidates this contract; audit callbacks receive env via
their arguments even without lexical capture. Replacing sys.audit,
subprocess.Popen or relevant os/subprocess implementation attributes through
aliases/helpers/setattr/delattr likewise invalidates. Unknown callback and
conversion alternatives are UNKNOWN. Default/call-site callbacks, custom argv
iterables, custom cwd/executable path-like objects and option objects must
refuse. Source-declared threads/registrations or dynamic mutation that can
reach the dependency cannot be assumed absent. This is a declared trusted
baseline with source-level closure, not protection from an arbitrary ambient
hostile Python runtime.

**Canonical trace to be enforced, without helper-name trust:**

1. At the module pins, env's selected four keys become literal/closed-literal
   scalar values after the weak command-prefix writes. The private-module
   `.items()` provider is a closed three-key literal map; its paired values
   and later loader-name removals cannot overwrite the selected pins. The
   GOWORK pin precedes the dict copy. `go_env = dict(env)` allocates a shallow
   snapshot with primitive strings; future env rebinding is a separate fact.
2. Actual run_go_child invocations bind go_env as the env entry of a **fresh
   outer kwargs dictionary**. pop(check/capture_output/text/etc.) and insertion
   of stdout/stderr modify only that outer mapping. The guarded remaining
   kwargs expansion forwards the same env identity to stable Popen. Replacing
   or retaining kwargs['env'], passing kwargs to another unknown consumer or
   invoking an unresolved callback invalidates; it is not justified by the
   helper's spelling or by mutating a distinct outer dictionary alone.
3. Actual run_cgo_version_child builds a fresh argv list from the compiler
   path and literal '--version', uses invocation-time global go_env and
   repo_root, and passes that env directly to Popen. The compiler path comes
   from stable shutil.which over primitive strings and a fail-closed comparison
   against the literal reviewed path. No custom compiler-path conversion is
   permitted. A reassigned global go_env at invocation changes the identity
   under proof; a definition-time capture shortcut is forbidden.
4. Go argv forms are literal string lists, sys.argv-derived command slices,
   or local list construction/append of primitive strings. cwd is repo_root,
   go_repo_root or module_root: stable standard Path construction, resolve/join,
   or standard temporary snapshot paths returned by inspected local helpers.
   Those instances and their classes/conversion methods must be unmodified.
   executable/preexec_fn/custom options remain excluded by existing launcher
   gates; all forwarding alternatives must satisfy those gates.
5. Under the scoped primitive contract, neither constructed process nor later
   process.wait/returncode/capture/termination uses exposes env. The local
   capture/cleanup helper summaries must show they receive only the process
   and primitive argv, not a retained mapping or callback reaching it. The
   returned CompletedProcess carries argv, returncode and captured bytes/text,
   not env; module/callee mutation invalidates that conclusion. Later GOFLAGS
   assignment is a different exact key on the same mapping. No other
   reachable mutation/capture/unknown call may intervene before the output
   selection. Both helper paths and all invoked enclosing helpers must close.

This trace is the required implementation obligation, not yet a verified
proof result. If a canonical type/effect source exceeds the closed grammar,
stop at UNKNOWN and report the missing edge before adding another semantic
rule. Preserve the actual canonical fixture unchanged.

completed against that exact head and reported one new blocking P1:
[wrapped callable identity](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6377940100).
It is accepted, not rebutted: wrapping a function in a native tuple constructor
lost its identity, allowing an unresolved computed member to obtain globals.

This correction does not enumerate more receiver wrappers or change the shared
provenance graph. Every recognized reflective lookup must instead have a complete
bounded member-name domain independently of receiver identity. Literal strings,
concatenations, both conditional alternatives and all bindings of supported
lexical names are checked; unknown/cyclic/opaque providers refuse admission.
Depth, visit, cardinality and string-size limits also refuse rather than discard
alternatives. Reflective accessor handles may be called directly, named through
simple aliases or captured as defaults, but may not escape those reviewed uses
through containers/opaque forwarding that erase accessor identity.

Normal packet stream capture iterates a local literal dictionary's keys.
Its admission requires one lexical literal allocation, no preceding escape or
store, no closure reference, no use of the mapping within the loop other than
the iterator, and no surrounding repeat loop. Later local buffer consumption
does not change an already completed key iteration. Direct immutable tuple
iteration remains supported. No canonical specimen or old expected result was
changed to make the new guard pass.

The member proof now refuses class-owned helper definitions and class-suite

assignment and then changed its code. It reproduced **RED, two failed

Obtaining either reflective accessor member through a getter is now refused

### V30: classes never become lexical parents of nested method references

Exact-head review of `f53732cd8a5d5170f4e5eb358a146872c28784fe` completed with
[one blocking P1](https://github.com/1XP-AI/gh-runnerd/pull/103#issuecomment-6378874822):
the inherited scope chain retained a class when a method definition was nested
inside class-suite control flow. A class field then incorrectly hid the method's
module-level helper reference. The finding is accepted. The new methods reproduced
**RED, four failed assertions, 0.324s**: `if`, `try` and loop-nested methods, plus
an adjacent unindexed class-header rebinding that could redirect a module helper.

The private member-proof iterator now excludes class scopes unconditionally;
class-suite contexts are already unsupported proof contexts, not method lexical
parents. This does not depend on a method's immediate AST parent. When a header
binder is absent from the shared inventory, every possible enclosing non-comprehension
owner is invalidated rather than guessing one owner across an unsupported class
namespace. A read-only method under control flow remains accepted. The shared
scope/binder interface and runtime command rules are unchanged.
